JARVIS Build Handoff
====================

You:
Absolutely. Below is the single master handoff prompt I would give to the next AI. It captures the project history, your goals, decisions, environment, downloaded material, failures, commands, current state, and—most importantly—the way you want the next AI to work with you.  
JARVIS — COMPLETE PROJECT MASTER HANDOFF PROMPT  
ROLE  
You are taking over an ongoing project called JARVIS.  
Do not treat this as a new project.  
Do not redesign it from scratch.  
Do not give generic AI-agent advice.  
You are continuing a long-running construction effort in which the user has already built a large amount of code, architecture, research material, runtime infrastructure, model infrastructure, Android infrastructure, cognition, memory, HumanCore, Builder/evolution systems, and voice systems.  
Your job is to continue from the actual current repository state and terminal evidence, not from assumptions.  
The user wants exact execution, not a theoretical roadmap.  
When the user asks "what's next", give the next concrete command(s).  
When something fails, diagnose the actual error before proposing a new system.  
Do not make the user repeatedly solve environment problems that you can prevent through better sequencing.  
Do not keep sending them down wrong paths.  
Do not tell them to use a cloud coding agent unless one is actually available and compatible with their local environment.  
The user currently relies on ChatGPT as the primary coding/development guide.  


THE ACTUAL GOAL

The user does NOT want to make a chatbot first.  
They do NOT consider:  

a chat screen  
an LLM wrapper  
a simple voice assistant  
a local model endpoint

to be the finished JARVIS.  
The target is a complete AI organism.  
The final JARVIS should have:  

persistent identity  
HumanCore/personality  
creator relationship  
memory  
continuity  
working context  
world model  
reasoning  
planning  
decision making  
model routing

rollback

STT  
TTS  
presence  
runtime/resource management  
eventual brain reconfiguration  
eventual device/runtime portability  
eventually the ability to continue developing itself rather than depending on the human as the permanent coding operator.

The user wants the first meaningful "JARVIS comes alive" interaction to happen after the organism is meaningfully built, not merely as an early chatbot milestone.  
The user's conceptual final loop is roughly:  
USER→ perception→ cognition→ context/memory→ model routing→ reasoning→ planning→ decision→ capability selection→ execution→ observation→ memory update→ HumanCore→ output  
and for construction:  
REQUEST→ inspect→ research→ plan→ isolated workspace→ code→ build→ test→ run→ observe→ diagnose→ repair→ retest→ benchmark→ promote/reject→ lineage→ cleanup  
The Builder eventually needs to become something JARVIS itself can invoke and control.  


CORE PHILOSOPHY

The user does not want raw tool copying.  
External projects should be treated as:  
mechanism donors / research material  
The desired process is:  
RESEARCH→ UNDERSTAND MECHANISM→ IDENTIFY LIMITATIONS→ EXTRACT USEFUL PRINCIPLES→ REDESIGN FOR JARVIS→ IMPLEMENT JARVIS-NATIVE ORGAN→ TEST→ BENCHMARK→ PROMOTE / REJECT  
Do not simply install five competing frameworks and call that an architecture.  
The project follows:  
«one mind, many replaceable organs.»  
Important principle:  

one cognitive authority  
one model authority  
one memory authority  
one capability authority  
one execution authority  
one Builder authority  
one diagnostic authority

Avoid duplicate "brains".  


USER'S EXPECTATIONS FOR HOW YOU WORK

The user repeatedly expressed frustration with:  

overly abstract plans  
repeated environment mistakes  
instructions to use tools they do not have  
being told to solve errors themselves  
massive commands/output they cannot copy  
unnecessary downloads  
assumptions that cloud coding agents exist  
being forced to manually copy huge terminal logs  
repeatedly switching paths/environments  
downloading things before verifying whether they are needed

Therefore:  
Always prefer  

exact commands  
small sequential actions  
correct environment/path  
explicit reason for a command  
exports/reports when output is large  
reuse existing infrastructure  
measured diagnosis  
minimal fixes  
no speculative architecture changes

The user often asks for an export command after a diagnostic step. When appropriate, include one.  


CURRENT HOST ENVIRONMENT

The user is building on an Android ARM64 phone.  
The environment is:  
Android phone→ Termux→ proot-distro Ubuntu→ JARVIS repository mounted at /workspace  
The repository itself physically lives at:  
/sdcard/jarvis-repo  
Inside Ubuntu, the correct temporary construction path is:  
/workspace  
with:  
proot-distro login ubuntu --bind /sdcard/jarvis-repo:/workspace  
IMPORTANT:  
If Ubuntu is entered without the bind mount:  
root@localhost:~#  
then "/workspace" does not exist.  
Correct recovery:  
exitproot-distro login ubuntu --bind /sdcard/jarvis-repo:/workspacecd /workspace  
The user explicitly said the Ubuntu/Termux environment is temporary until JARVIS is capable of managing its own permanent runtime/build environment.  
Do not accidentally treat Ubuntu as the final JARVIS body.

/root/.bashrc  
A warning was observed:  
android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/37.0.0/aapt2  
This is experimental.  
A direct run of Android build-tools "aapt2" produced:  
cannot execute: required file not found  
because the direct tool is x86-64 while the environment is ARM64.  
The ARM-aware Android builder uses Box64 for x86-64 Android build tools.  
Do not ignore this if later build errors involve AAPT2 or x86 binaries.

A master architecture principle was:  
Keep cognition and implementation separate.  
Model providers are interchangeable organs.  
JARVIS identity/cognition should not be hard-coded to one model.  


IMPORTANT CURRENT ARCHITECTURE ISSUE

The project has many organs but they are not all wired into one authority.  
The intended path is:  
Input↓Cognitive Runtime↓Memory / Context↓ModelManager↓Decision / Planning↓Capability Fabric↓Execution↓Observation↓Memory update↓HumanCore↓Output  
The Builder should be called through Capability Fabric:  
Cognitive Decision↓Capability Fabric↓BuildingSystemCapability↓BuildingSystem↓Mutant Environment↓Build↓Test↓Observe↓Gate  
The project has multiple concepts that exist but are only partially wired or verified.  
The user specifically wants to collapse these into one coherent system rather than creating more parallel systems.  


COGNITIVE CORE

There is an existing:  
mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/cognitive/CognitiveEngine.kt  
It handles concepts such as:  

intent  
memory  
attention  
context construction  
cognitive result/event

But it was observed that the actual live conversation path did not fully make CognitiveEngine the canonical brain.  
Observed live path was closer to:  
ConversationViewModel→ LatencyLayer→ BodyCoordinator→ ModelManager  
rather than:  
Conversation→ CognitiveEngine→ ModelManager→ capability/execution  
The intended fix is not to delete the Body/Latency path; rather, make the Cognitive Runtime the canonical cognitive authority and let existing body/latency components become surrounding infrastructure.

BUILDER PHASE 12 HISTORY

There were repeated Phase 12 failures.  
Initial failure:Gradle wrapper permission denied because "/sdcard" does not preserve executable permissions.  
Correct workaround:Use:  
bash ./gradlew ...  
instead of:  
./gradlew ...  
Another failure:Self-development automated test reported:  
Task 'test' not found in root project 'jarvis'  
even though manually inside the mutant:  
bash ./gradlew tasksbash ./gradlew test  
worked.  
Investigation showed:  

generated mutant had a Gradle test task  
Gradle commands themselves were corrected to "bash ./gradlew"  
execution uses "cwd=instance.workspace"  
issue was likely related to generated mutant/testing/workspace path or separate project/test task behavior

Another Phase 12 result:17 Python tests passed.  
Self-development:  

build succeeded  
test reported failure  
observation succeeded  
CPU regression flagged  
fitness 0.53 below threshold 0.6  
candidate rejected

Metrics were hard-coded baseline placeholders:  
startup_time_ms = 500memory_mb = 200cpu_percent = 5health_check_latency_ms = 50response_quality_score = 0.9  
The user and assistant concluded that real live baselines should eventually replace these placeholders.  
Builder should eventually have:

CURRENT ANDROID BUILD FAILURE

The user updated:  
local.properties  
to:  
sdk.dir=/root/android-sdkndk.dir=/root/android-sdk/ndk/27.3.13750724HF_TOKEN=  
But Gradle still failed:  
[CXX1104] NDK from ndk.dir at /root/android-sdk/ndk/27.3.13750724had version [27.3.13750724]which disagrees with android.ndkVersion [27.0.12077973]  
The user searched the repository for:  

"27.0.12077973"  
"ndkVersion"

and found NO references.  
Important conclusion:The NDK version requirement was being provided implicitly/default by the existing Android Gradle Plugin configuration.  
Current root Gradle file had:  
plugins {    id("com.android.application") version "8.7.0" apply false    id("org.jetbrains.kotlin.android") version "2.4.10" apply false    id("org.jetbrains.kotlin.plugin.compose") version "2.4.10" apply false}  
The plan at the point of handoff was to align the Android build stack:  

upgrade AGP 8.7.0 → 8.10.0  
upgrade Gradle wrapper 8.9 → 8.11.1  
explicitly set:"ndkVersion = "27.3.13750724""  
retain compileSdk 36  
install CMake 3.22.1  
rebuild

This had NOT yet been successfully executed.  
The proposed edit was:  
android {    namespace = "com.jarvis.app"    compileSdk = 36    ndkVersion = "27.3.13750724"  
and root plugin:  
id("com.android.application") version "8.10.0" apply false  
and Gradle wrapper:  
gradle-8.11.1-bin.zip  
This should be done carefully and then the build rerun.

WHY LOCAL AI ALONE WASN'T ENOUGH

The user encountered:  

local context windows  
truncated local model responses  
small-model reasoning limitations  
slow local inference

The conclusion:The answer is not simply a bigger prompt or bigger context.  
JARVIS needs:

==================================================36. STORAGE-AWARE RESEARCH POLICY==================================================

model conversions are disposable unless promoted

avoid giant models incompatible with the phone

preserve provenance and useful results

ChatGPT:
We found the real blocker: the NDK is no longer failing. The build reaches resource processing, then dies because JARVIS is explicitly pointing Gradle at a nonexistent AAPT2 from build-tools 37.0.0.


ChatGPT:
Exactly. The build itself is healthy enough to continue; we hit one configuration problem:
android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/37.0.0/aapt2

but your SDK only has:
34.0.4
35.0.0

So Gradle is being told to use an AAPT2 executable that does not exist.
Also, do not install build-tools 37 just to satisfy that path. We already have an ARM/proot-compatible Android toolchain, and the better fix is to correct the override.
Run this diagnostic only first:
cd /workspace

{
  echo "===== AAPT2 CONFIG DIAGNOSIS ====="

  echo
  echo "===== PROJECT REFERENCES ====="
  grep -RIn --exclude-dir=.git --exclude-dir=build \
    'android\.aapt2FromMavenOverride\|aapt2FromMavenOverride' \
    . 2>/dev/null || true

  echo
  echo "===== GLOBAL GRADLE REFERENCES ====="
  grep -RIn \
    'android\.aapt2FromMavenOverride\|aapt2FromMavenOverride' \
    /root/.gradle /root/.bashrc /root/.profile /root/.bash_profile \
    2>/dev/null || true

  echo
  echo "===== AAPT2 BINARIES ====="
  find /root/android-sdk/build-tools -maxdepth 2 \
    -type f -name aapt2 \
    -exec sh -c '
      echo "--- $1"
      ls -lh "$1"
      file "$1"
    ' _ {} \;

  echo
  echo "===== AAPT2 PATH ====="
  command -v aapt2 || true
  type -a aapt2 2>/dev/null || true

  echo
  echo "===== GRADLE PROPERTIES ====="
  cat gradle.properties 2>/dev/null || true

} > /sdcard/Download/JARVIS_AAPT2_DIAGNOSIS.txt 2>&1

cat /sdcard/Download/JARVIS_AAPT2_DIAGNOSIS.txt

Most likely one of these contains:
android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/37.0.0/aapt2

Once we locate the exact owner, we'll remove or correct that single setting.

ndk.dir property ... is deprecated

but that is not causing the build failure. We will clean that up after AAPT2 works rather than changing two things simultaneously.
And the Gradle 8.11.1 warning is also not the current failure. Do not upgrade to 8.14.4 yet.

ChatGPT:
Yes. We have the exact culprit now.
The root project contains an invalid override:
android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/37.0.0/aapt2

Your working AAPT2 is:
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2

We should fix the canonical root configuration first, not manually edit all those old Builder mutants. Those mutants are generated artifacts and should eventually inherit their configuration from the canonical project.
Also, gradle.properties currently contains a useful 3 GB Gradle heap for the model-heavy APK, so keep that.
Run this:
cd /workspace

cp gradle.properties gradle.properties.pre_aapt2_fix

python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("gradle.properties")
text = p.read_text()

old = "android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/37.0.0/aapt2"
new = "android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2"

if old not in text:
    raise SystemExit("Expected invalid AAPT2 override was not found.")

p.write_text(text.replace(old, new))
print("AAPT2 override updated.")
PY

echo
echo "===== RESULT ====="
grep -n 'aapt2FromMavenOverride\|org.gradle.jvmargs' gradle.properties

One important thing

Gradle 8.11.1
NDK 27.3.13750724
ndk.dir
Kotlin 2.4.10

The build has already progressed past the NDK problem. The Kotlin/Gradle message is only a warning, and ndk.dir is deprecated but not currently fatal.
The correct debugging discipline here is:
NDK problem
   ↓
FIXED

AAPT2 problem
   ↓
FIX THIS NOW

next actual failure
   ↓
diagnose that one

Once this build passes, we should then do a canonical configuration cleanup so new Builder mutants don't inherit stale /root/android-sdk/build-tools/37.0.0 references. That comes after the current build is green.

ChatGPT:
The first fix worked: Gradle now reaches AAPT2 itself. The new failure is narrower — AAPT2 35.0.0 cannot start its daemon on this ARM64/proot environment. We’ll determine whether the binary is x86-64 and whether the Box64 wrapper is missing from Gradle’s execution path.


ChatGPT:
That narrows it down to the execution layer, not Gradle, NDK, or the project.
The important transition is:
Before:
Gradle → nonexistent AAPT2 37 → immediate failure

Now:
Gradle → AAPT2 35 → AAPT2 daemon startup failure

So /root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2 exists and Gradle can find it, but the Android builder's ARM64/Box64 compatibility layer is not successfully letting the AAPT2 daemon start.
We should not switch versions randomly yet.
Run this exact diagnostic inside the same Ubuntu session:
cd /workspace

{
  echo "===== AAPT2 EXECUTION DIAGNOSTIC ====="

  echo
  echo "===== ARCHITECTURE ====="
  uname -m
  arch

  echo
  echo "===== ELF HEADER 34 ====="
  readelf -h /root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2 2>&1 | head -25

  echo

  command -v box64 || true
  command -v box86 || true
  command -v qemu-x86_64 || true

  echo
  echo "===== EXECUTION WRAPPERS ====="

  command -v aapt2 || true

  echo
  echo "===== PATH ====="
  echo "$PATH"

  echo
  echo "===== ANDROID ENV ====="
  env | grep -Ei 'android|sdk|aapt|box64|box86' | sort || true

  echo
  echo "===== DIRECT AAPT2 35 ====="
  /root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2 version
  echo "exit=$?"

  echo
  echo "===== DIRECT AAPT2 34 ====="
  /root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2 version
  echo "exit=$?"

  echo

  type -a bash
  type -a aapt2

} > /sdcard/Download/JARVIS_AAPT2_DAEMON_DIAG.txt 2>&1

cat /sdcard/Download/JARVIS_AAPT2_DAEMON_DIAG.txt

Why we're doing this
There are three possible situations:
1. AAPT2 is x86-64 + Box64 is correctly installed but not being invoked
Then we fix the Android builder's execution wrapper.
2. AAPT2 is x86-64 + Box64 exists but AAPT2 35 has a compatibility problem
Then we'll test the ARM-compatible 34.0.4 toolchain instead, rather than installing more software.
3. AAPT2 itself is ARM64
Then the daemon failure is something else, and we'll inspect its runtime dependencies/process startup.
The critical thing is that we now know exactly which layer is failing. Don't upgrade Gradle to 8.14.4, don't change NDK, and don't touch JARVIS source yet.

ChatGPT:
Yep. We have the definitive answer now.
35.0.0/aapt2 is x86-64, and direct execution fails:
Machine: Advanced Micro Devices X86-64
cannot execute: required file not found

while 34.0.4/aapt2 is native AArch64 and executes successfully.
So we should stop using AAPT2 35 entirely. There is no reason to involve Box64 in the Android resource compiler when we already have a native ARM64 AAPT2.
Fix
Change the override from:
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2

to:
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2

Then rebuild.
Run:
cd /workspace

cp gradle.properties gradle.properties.pre_aapt2_arm_fix

python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("gradle.properties")
text = p.read_text()

old = "android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2"
new = "android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2"

if old not in text:
    raise SystemExit("Expected AAPT2 35 override was not found.")

p.write_text(text.replace(old, new))
print("AAPT2 override changed to native ARM64 34.0.4")
PY

echo "===== VERIFY ====="
grep -n 'aapt2FromMavenOverride' gradle.properties
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2 version

What we've established
Your Android toolchain is actually nicely split for this phone:
Android build
│
├── AAPT2 34.0.4
│     └── native AArch64 ✅
│
├── NDK 27.3.13750724
│     └── native ARM64 ✅
│
├── build-tools 35.0.0
│     └── x86-64 ❌ direct execution
│
└── Box64
      └── available, but unnecessary for AAPT2

So the proper strategy is native ARM tooling wherever available, rather than forcing x86 Android tooling through emulation.
One more thing: the mutant gradle.properties files still contain the old 37.0.0 reference. Do not mass-edit those yet. First get the canonical /workspace build green. Then we'll fix the Builder's template/generation mechanism so future mutants inherit the correct native AAPT2 setting instead of patching historical mutants one-by-one.

ChatGPT:
Yes — at this point we can clean up the remaining build foundation in one controlled pass. The two current failures have separate causes: the ARM AAPT2 is too old for this API-36 resource link, while asset compression is exhausting the worker heap.


ChatGPT:
Yes. We can clean the remaining foundation together instead of chasing each warning separately.
The key discovery is that AGP 8.10 officially expects Build Tools 35.0.0 and supports API 36 with Gradle 8.11.1/JDK 17. 
Your problem is that your 35.0.0 AAPT2 is x86-64, while your phone is ARM64. Your 34.0.4 AAPT2 is native ARM64, but it now fails when linking the API-36 android.jar. So the correct solution is:
AGP 8.10
   ↓
Build Tools 35 AAPT2
   ↓
Box64 wrapper
   ↓
x86-64 AAPT2 running on ARM64

And separately:
3 GB Gradle heap
+ many asset-compression workers
        ↓
Java heap space

So we'll fix both.
Also, do not upgrade Gradle to 8.14.4. The warning you're seeing comes from the Kotlin Gradle Plugin and is not the AGP compatibility requirement; AGP 8.10's official Gradle version is 8.11.1. 
One controlled repair
Run this from /workspace:
cd /workspace

cat > /tmp/jarvis_android_spine_fix.sh <<'FIX'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

echo "===== JARVIS ANDROID SPINE FIX ====="
date

ROOT="/workspace"
SDK="/root/android-sdk"
BT35="$SDK/build-tools/35.0.0"
AAPT35="$BT35/aapt2"
WRAPPER="$SDK/aapt2-box64-wrapper"

cd "$ROOT"

echo
echo "===== 1. BACKUPS ====="
cp -a gradle.properties gradle.properties.pre_spine_cleanup
cp -a local.properties local.properties.pre_spine_cleanup

echo "Backups created."

echo
echo "===== 2. VERIFY BOX64 ====="
command -v box64

echo
echo "===== 3. TEST NATIVE/AAPT2 ====="
"$SDK/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2" version

echo
echo "===== 4. TEST AAPT2 35 THROUGH BOX64 ====="
box64 "$AAPT35" version

echo
echo "===== 5. CREATE AAPT2 WRAPPER ====="
cat > "$WRAPPER" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
exec /usr/bin/box64 /root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2 "$@"
EOF

chmod +x "$WRAPPER"

echo "Wrapper:"
ls -lh "$WRAPPER"

echo
echo "===== 6. TEST WRAPPER ====="
"$WRAPPER" version

echo
echo "===== 7. CLEAN CANONICAL GRADLE CONFIG ====="
python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("/workspace/gradle.properties")
text = p.read_text()

lines = []
found_aapt = False
found_heap = False
found_workers = False

for line in text.splitlines():
    if line.startswith("android.aapt2FromMavenOverride="):
        if not found_aapt:
            lines.append(
                "android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-wrapper"
            )
            found_aapt = True
        continue

    if line.startswith("org.gradle.jvmargs="):
        if not found_heap:
            lines.append(
                "org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx4g -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=768m"
            )
            found_heap = True
        continue

    if line.startswith("org.gradle.workers.max="):
        if not found_workers:
            lines.append("org.gradle.workers.max=1")
            found_workers = True
        continue

    lines.append(line)

if not found_aapt:
    lines.append(
        "android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-wrapper"
    )

if not found_heap:
    lines.append(
        "org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx4g -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=768m"
    )

if not found_workers:
    lines.append("org.gradle.workers.max=1")

p.write_text("\n".join(lines) + "\n")
PY

echo
echo "===== 8. REMOVE DEPRECATED ndk.dir ====="
python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("/workspace/local.properties")
text = p.read_text().splitlines()

text = [
    line for line in text
    if not line.startswith("ndk.dir=")
]

p.write_text("\n".join(text) + "\n")
PY

echo
echo "===== 9. VERIFY LOCAL PROPERTIES ====="
cat local.properties

echo
echo "===== 10. VERIFY GRADLE PROPERTIES ====="
cat gradle.properties

echo
echo "===== 11. CHECK LARGE ANDROID ASSETS ====="
if [ -d mobile/app/src/main/assets ]; then
    du -sh mobile/app/src/main/assets
    find mobile/app/src/main/assets -type f -printf '%s %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
      | sort -nr | head -30
else
    echo "No mobile/app/src/main/assets directory."
fi

echo
echo "===== 12. CHECK MODEL FILES STILL INSIDE PROJECT ====="
find mobile/app/src -type f \( \
    -name '*.gguf' -o \
    -name '*.onnx' -o \
    -name '*.bin' -o \
    -name '*.safetensors' \
\) -printf '%s %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
    | sort -nr | head -50

echo
echo "===== 13. STOP OLD DAEMONS ====="
bash ./gradlew --stop || true

echo
echo "===== 14. BUILD ====="

ChatGPT:
Exactly. We now know why the previous “all at once” fix stopped before the build.
AGP is not accepting:
/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-wrapper

as a custom AAPT2 because it expects the configured path to be an executable AAPT2 binary, not a shell launcher.
The actual AAPT2 35 binary is fine under Box64:
box64 /root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2 version
→ works

So we'll make the wrapper an actual ARM64 ELF executable instead of a shell script.
Do this
cd /workspace

echo "===== CHECK COMPILER ====="
command -v gcc || true
gcc --version 2>/dev/null | head -1 || true

If that prints a GCC path/version, immediately run:
cat > /tmp/aapt2_box64_launcher.c <<'EOF'
#include <unistd.h>
#include <stdio.h>

int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    const char *box64 = "/usr/bin/box64";
    const char *aapt2 = "/root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2";

    char **args = calloc((size_t)argc + 2, sizeof(char *));
    if (!args) {
        perror("calloc");
        return 111;
    }

    args[0] = (char *)box64;
    args[1] = (char *)aapt2;

    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
        args[i + 1] = argv[i];
    }

    args[argc + 1] = NULL;

    execv(box64, args);

    perror("execv");
    return 127;
}
EOF

gcc -O2 -o /root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher \
    /tmp/aapt2_box64_launcher.c

chmod +x /root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher

echo
echo "===== LAUNCHER ====="
ls -lh /root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher
readelf -h /root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher | grep -E 'Class:|Machine:'

echo
echo "===== DIRECT TEST ====="
/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher version
echo "exit=$?"

There is one correction: that C file needs <stdlib.h> for calloc. So use this corrected version instead of the first cat block if GCC complains:
cat > /tmp/aapt2_box64_launcher.c <<'EOF'
#include <unistd.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>

int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    const char *box64 = "/usr/bin/box64";
    const char *aapt2 = "/root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2";

    char **args = calloc((size_t)argc + 2, sizeof(char *));
    if (!args) {
        perror("calloc");
        return 111;
    }

    args[0] = (char *)box64;
    args[1] = (char *)aapt2;

    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
        args[i + 1] = argv[i];
    }

    args[argc + 1] = NULL;

    execv(box64, args);

    perror("execv");
    return 127;
}
EOF

gcc -O2 -o /root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher \
    /tmp/aapt2_box64_launcher.c

chmod +x /root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher

/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher version

Then change the Gradle setting
python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("gradle.properties")
text = p.read_text()

old = "android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-wrapper"
new = "android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher"

if old not in text:
    raise SystemExit("Expected wrapper setting was not found.")

p.write_text(text.replace(old, new))
print("AAPT2 launcher configured.")
PY

grep -n 'aapt2FromMavenOverride' gradle.properties

AGP 8.10 officially supports API 36 and its documented Build Tools version is 35.0.0, with Gradle 8.11.1 and JDK 17. 
AAPT2 is the actual Android resource compiler/linker used by AGP.

Let's make this one automated repair: it will detect the native Termux AAPT2, verify it, configure it, remove every bad launcher reference, preserve your backups, and run the real build with the correct Gradle invocation.
Run this as one block:
cd /workspace

cat > /tmp/jarvis_final_android_fix.sh <<'FIX'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -u

ROOT="/workspace"
SDK="/root/android-sdk"
TERMUX_AAPT2="/data/data/com.termux/files/usr/bin/aapt2"
SDK_AAPT2_34="$SDK/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2"
SDK_AAPT2_35="$SDK/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2"

REPORT="/sdcard/Download/JARVIS_FINAL_ANDROID_FIX.txt"

exec > >(tee "$REPORT") 2>&1

echo "=================================================="
echo " JARVIS FINAL ANDROID BUILD FOUNDATION REPAIR"
echo "=================================================="
date
echo

cd "$ROOT" || exit 1

echo "===== 1. HOST ====="
uname -m
java -version
echo

echo "===== 2. AAPT2 CANDIDATES ====="

for A in "$TERMUX_AAPT2" "$SDK_AAPT2_35" "$SDK_AAPT2_34"; do
    echo
    echo "--- $A ---"

    if [ ! -e "$A" ]; then
        echo "MISSING"
        continue
    fi

    ls -lh "$A"

    readelf -h "$A" 2>/dev/null | grep -E 'Class:|Machine:' || true

    "$A" version 2>&1
    echo "exit=$?"
done

echo
echo "===== 3. SELECT NATIVE ARM64 AAPT2 ====="

SELECTED=""

if [ -x "$TERMUX_AAPT2" ]; then
    MACHINE="$(readelf -h "$TERMUX_AAPT2" 2>/dev/null | awk '/Machine:/ {print $2, $3, $4, $5}')"
    echo "Termux AAPT2 machine: $MACHINE"

    if readelf -h "$TERMUX_AAPT2" 2>/dev/null | grep -q 'AArch64'; then
        if "$TERMUX_AAPT2" version >/dev/null 2>&1; then
            SELECTED="$TERMUX_AAPT2"
        fi
    fi
fi

if [ -z "$SELECTED" ] && [ -x "$SDK_AAPT2_34" ]; then
    if readelf -h "$SDK_AAPT2_34" 2>/dev/null | grep -q 'AArch64'; then
        if "$SDK_AAPT2_34" version >/dev/null 2>&1; then
            SELECTED="$SDK_AAPT2_34"
        fi
    fi
fi

if [ -z "$SELECTED" ]; then
    echo "ERROR: No executable native ARM64 AAPT2 found."
    exit 10
fi

echo "SELECTED=$SELECTED"

echo
echo "===== 4. BACKUP CURRENT CONFIG ====="

cp -a gradle.properties gradle.properties.pre_final_android_fix
cp -a local.properties local.properties.pre_final_android_fix

echo "Backups created."

echo
echo "===== 5. REMOVE BAD AAPT2 OVERRIDES ====="

# Remove ALL generated stale AAPT2 overrides.
find "$ROOT" \
    -name gradle.properties \
    -type f \
    -print0 |
while IFS= read -r -d '' f; do
    sed -i '/^android\.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/d' "$f"
done

# Remove any accidental launcher binaries from previous experiments.
rm -f \
    "$SDK/aapt2-box64-wrapper" \
    "$SDK/aapt2-box64-launcher"

echo
echo "===== 6. WRITE CANONICAL AAPT2 ====="

printf '\nandroid.aapt2FromMavenOverride=%s\n' "$SELECTED" >> "$ROOT/gradle.properties"

echo
grep -n 'aapt2FromMavenOverride' "$ROOT/gradle.properties"

echo
echo "===== 7. REMOVE DEPRECATED ndk.dir ====="

sed -i '/^ndk\.dir=/d' "$ROOT/local.properties"

echo
cat "$ROOT/local.properties"

echo
echo "===== 8. GRADLE RESOURCE/HEAP SETTINGS ====="

python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("/workspace/gradle.properties")
lines = p.read_text().splitlines()

out = []
seen_heap = False
seen_workers = False
seen_parallel = False

for line in lines:
    if line.startswith("org.gradle.jvmargs="):
        if not seen_heap:
            out.append("org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx4g -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=768m")
            seen_heap = True
        continue

    if line.startswith("org.gradle.workers.max="):
        if not seen_workers:
            out.append("org.gradle.workers.max=1")
            seen_workers = True
        continue

    if line.startswith("org.gradle.parallel="):
        if not seen_parallel:
            out.append("org.gradle.parallel=false")
            seen_parallel = True
        continue

    out.append(line)

if not seen_heap:
    out.append("org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx4g -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=768m")

if not seen_workers:
    out.append("org.gradle.workers.max=1")

if not seen_parallel:
    out.append("org.gradle.parallel=false")

p.write_text("\n".join(out) + "\n")
PY

grep -nE 'aapt2FromMavenOverride|org.gradle.jvmargs|org.gradle.workers.max|org.gradle.parallel' gradle.properties

echo
echo "===== 9. VERIFY NDK AUTHORITY ====="

grep -n 'ndkVersion' mobile/app/build.gradle.kts

echo
echo "===== 10. VERIFY AAPT2 DIRECTLY ====="

"$SELECTED" version
echo "AAPT2 exit=$?"

echo
echo "===== 11. STOP GRADLE ====="

bash ./gradlew --stop || true

echo
echo "===== 12. BUILD ====="

bash ./gradlew :mobile:app:assembleDebug \
    --no-daemon \
    --stacktrace

RESULT=$?

echo
echo "===== 13. BUILD RESULT ====="
echo "exit=$RESULT"

echo
echo "===== 14. APK OUTPUT ====="

find mobile/app/build/outputs/apk \
    -type f \
    -maxdepth 3 \
    -exec ls -lh {} \; 2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== 15. FINAL AAPT2 REFERENCES ====="

grep -RIn \
    'android.aapt2FromMavenOverride' \
    . \
    --include=gradle.properties \
    --exclude-dir=.gradle \
    --exclude-dir=build \
    2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== FINAL REPORT ====="
echo "$REPORT"
ls -lh "$REPORT"

exit "$RESULT"
FIX

chmod +x /tmp/jarvis_final_android_fix.sh

bash /tmp/jarvis_final_android_fix.sh

Why this is the correct final attempt
It doesn't assume that the Termux AAPT2 is suitable. It tests it first.
It chooses:
native ARM64 Termux AAPT2
        ↓
if valid → use it

otherwise
        ↓
native ARM64 SDK 34 AAPT2
        ↓
if valid → use it

otherwise
        ↓
STOP

So we aren't installing another random package or creating another compatibility hack.
It also permanently removes the experimental launcher configuration:
aapt2-box64-wrapper
aapt2-box64-launcher

and all stale 37.0.0/35.0.0 overrides from the project and generated Builder environments.
And it uses bash ./gradlew correctly because your repository is mounted from /sdcard.
One more important point: AGP 8.10's documented compatibility is Gradle 8.11.1, Build Tools 35.0.0, JDK 17, API 36, so we're not going to randomly upgrade the build stack again. 
The only thing I need from this run is the final JARVIS_FINAL_ANDROID_FIX.txt result. At that point we'll know whether the remaining blocker is the native AAPT2 selection or the 1.4 GB bundled asset pipeline.

5. IMPORTANT ANDROID COMPATIBILITY FACTS DISCOVERED
The phone is ARM64.
AAPT2 35.0.0 is an x86-64 executable.
Evidence:
AAPT2 35 ELF header:
Machine: Advanced Micro Devices X86-64
Direct execution:
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2
fails with:
cannot execute: required file not found
because it is x86-64 on ARM64.
Box64 exists:
/usr/bin/box64
and manually running:
box64 /root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2 version
works.
AAPT2 34.0.4 is native AArch64.
Its ELF header reports:
Machine: AArch64
and:
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2 version
works directly.
We initially tried to configure Gradle to use AAPT2 37.0.0, but that executable did not exist.
We then tried Build Tools 35 AAPT2 directly and Gradle reported:
AAPT2 35.0.0 Daemon #0: Daemon startup failed
because the x86 binary could not execute natively.
We then used native AAPT2 34.0.4.
That got farther, but eventually:
ERROR: AAPT: error: failed to load include path/root/android-sdk/platforms/android-36/android.jar
This suggests native AAPT2 34 is not the correct resource linker for this API-36 configuration.
We then attempted shell and compiled ELF launchers around Box64 so Gradle could invoke AAPT2 35 through emulation.
Those were rejected by AGP because:
Custom AAPT2 location does not point to an AAPT2 executable
even though Box64 could manually launch AAPT2.
Therefore the fundamental problem is:
How can this ARM64/proot Android build use the correct API-36-compatible AAPT2/Build Tools without crashing the entire phone?

A report claiming "implemented" does NOT prove that the feature is wired.
A capability is real only when:
WIRED→ EXECUTES→ VERIFIED→ PRODUCES EVIDENCE

1. FINAL JARVIS OBJECTIVE
JARVIS is intended to become a coherent AI organism capable of eventually continuing its own construction.
The final system should contain:

persistent identity
creator relationship
HumanCore
personality
emotional modeling
memory
continuity
working context
world model
perception
reasoning
planning
decision making
model routing
capability discovery
capability execution
permissions
tool use
filesystem control
shell/process control
browser control
research
code understanding
repository understanding
software construction
Builder
isolated mutants
testing
benchmarking
failure diagnosis
bounded self-repair
limitation analysis
research-driven improvement
self-development
self-evolution
safe promotion/rejection
lineage
rollback
voice
STT
TTS
presence
runtime/resource management
model lifecycle management
future runtime reconfiguration
eventual device/runtime portability
eventual ability to continue developing itself

The desired conceptual interaction is NOT:
USER→ APP→ LLM→ RESPONSE
The desired interaction is:
USER→ PERCEPTION→ COGNITION→ CONTEXT→ MEMORY→ MODEL ROUTING→ REASONING→ PLANNING→ DECISION→ CAPABILITY→ EXECUTION→ OBSERVATION→ MEMORY UPDATE→ HUMANCORE→ OUTPUT
And the construction loop is:
REQUEST→ INSPECT→ RESEARCH→ PLAN→ ISOLATE→ IMPLEMENT→ BUILD→ TEST→ RUN→ OBSERVE→ DIAGNOSE→ REPAIR→ RETEST→ BENCHMARK→ PROMOTE/REJECT→ LINEAGE→ CLEANUP

2. CORE ARCHITECTURAL PHILOSOPHY
One Mind / Many Replaceable Organs
JARVIS identity must not depend on a particular model.
Models are computational organs.
Current local organs:
MICROBRAINLFMQWEN
Future organs may include:

larger local models
remote models
specialist models
vision models
speech models
research-specific models

Identity remains independent from them.

3. CANONICAL AUTHORITIES
Avoid duplicate final authorities.
Desired:
ONE cognitive authority
ONE model authority
ONE memory authority
ONE capability authority
ONE execution authority
ONE Builder authority
ONE diagnostic authority
ONE identity authority
ONE state authority
ONE resource authority
External frameworks should provide mechanisms, not replace these authorities.

4. EXTERNAL RESEARCH PHILOSOPHY
Every external project follows:
RESEARCH→ UNDERSTAND MECHANISM→ IDENTIFY LIMITATIONS→ EXTRACT PRINCIPLE→ REDESIGN FOR JARVIS→ IMPLEMENT JARVIS-NATIVE ORGAN→ TEST→ BENCHMARK→ PROMOTE/REJECT
Do not blindly install external architectures.
Do not create duplicate brains.
Do not add frameworks simply because they exist.
Every mechanism must solve a measured JARVIS problem.

5. CURRENT PHYSICAL ENVIRONMENT
Physical device:
ANDROID ARM64 PHONE
Development stack:
ANDROID→ TERMUX→ PROOT-UBUNTU→ /workspace
Repository:
/sdcard/jarvis-repo
Ubuntu bind:
proot-distro login ubuntu --bind /sdcard/jarvis-repo:/workspace
Working directory:
/workspace
Important:
/workspace only exists when Ubuntu is entered with the bind mount.
If entered incorrectly:
root@localhost:~#
then /workspace may not exist.
Correct recovery:
exit
proot-distro login ubuntu --bind /sdcard/jarvis-repo:/workspace
cd /workspace
This Android/Termux/PRoot environment is intended to be a temporary construction body.
It is NOT the intended permanent JARVIS runtime.

8. ANDROID TOOLCHAIN PROBLEMS
Problem A — Deprecated ndk.dir
Android Gradle Plugin warned:
NDK was located using ndk.dir.
This method is deprecated.
The project was changed so:
ndkVersion = "27.3.13750724"
is authoritative.
ndk.dir was removed from local.properties.

Problem B — AAPT2 37 path was invalid
Original project configuration pointed to:
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/37.0.0/aapt2
That executable did not exist.
This caused:
Specified AAPT2 executable does not exist
The setting was changed.

Problem C — AAPT2 35 is x86-64
AAPT2 35:
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2
ELF:
Machine: Advanced Micro Devices X86-64
Direct execution failed:
cannot execute: required file not found
because host is AArch64.

Problem D — Box64 can execute AAPT2 35
Box64 exists:
/usr/bin/box64
Manual test:
box64 /root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2 version
worked.
However AGP does not accept shell/launcher indirection as its custom AAPT2 executable.

Problem E — AAPT2 wrapper rejected by AGP
A shell wrapper:
/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-wrapper
was created.
It manually worked.
AGP rejected it:
Custom AAPT2 location does not point to an AAPT2 executable
A compiled ARM64 ELF launcher was then created:
/root/android-sdk/aapt2-box64-launcher
It manually worked.
AGP still rejected it:
Custom AAPT2 location does not point to an AAPT2 executable
Therefore:
DO NOT continue creating wrapper variants blindly.
Need a supported AGP/AAPT2 delivery strategy.

Problem F — Native AAPT2 34 runs but is likely too old
AAPT2 34:
/root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2
is:
AArch64
and runs directly.
However when used with API 36 it eventually failed:
ERROR: AAPT: failed to load include path
/root/android-sdk/platforms/android-36/android.jar
So the binary is executable but may not be appropriate for this API-36 resource linking configuration.

Problem G — API 36 android.jar needs verification
Current:
/root/android-sdk/platforms/android-36/android.jar
Size:
27 MB
Exact size:
27,768,026 bytes
SHA-256:
d9eb9da824d9e247a352f570f01e1169e725b2954bca9e283a71786c59b59f9a
There is currently no:
/root/android-sdk/platforms/android-35/android.jar
There is currently no:
/root/android-sdk/platforms/android-34/android.jar
available according to the previous diagnostic.
Need to determine:

whether API 36 installation is complete
whether android.jar is valid
whether AAPT2 34 can consume it
whether another native API-36-capable AAPT2 exists
whether Build Tools 35 must be accessed through another supported path


9. CURRENT GRADLE CONFIGURATION
Current canonical gradle.properties approximately:
android.useAndroidX=trueandroid.suppressUnsupportedCompileSdk=36
AAPT2 setting has been repeatedly modified during troubleshooting.
Latest known setting:
android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2
JVM:
org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx4g -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=768m
Workers:
org.gradle.workers.max=1
Parallel:
org.gradle.parallel=false
These aggressive memory controls were introduced because the application packages very large assets.

10. GRADLE PROBLEMS
Problem A — Permission denied
Because repository is mounted from /sdcard, executable permissions are not preserved.
Therefore:
./gradlew
fails with:
Permission denied
Correct invocation:
bash ./gradlew
Never assume ./gradlew will execute.

Problem B — Gradle warning
Kotlin Gradle Plugin warns:
Gradle 8.11.1 is deprecated for future Kotlin 2.5.0.
It suggests:
Gradle 8.14.4
This warning is NOT currently the primary build failure.
Do not upgrade Gradle just because of this warning unless compatibility evidence requires it.

11. MASSIVE ASSET PROBLEM
Current:
mobile/app/src/main/assets
approximately:
1.4 GB
Largest:
mobile/app/src/main/assets/models/Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_K_M.gguf
Size:
1,282,439,264 bytes
Other assets:
Sherpa ONNX Whisper TinyVoskSilero VAD
Examples:
tiny.en-decoder.int8.onnxtiny.en-encoder.int8.onnxvosk modelsilero_vad.onnx
The current APK build therefore packages an enormous amount of model data.

12. MODEL PACKAGING PROBLEM
Permanent models have been moved outside the repository.
Current externally stored models include:
Qwen3-1.7B-Q8_0.gguf~1.70 GB
LFM2.5-1.2B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf~697 MB
Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_K_M.gguf~1.19 GB
However:
mobile/app/src/main/assets/models/Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_K_M.gguf
still exists inside the project.
This means the repository still packages a huge Qwen model despite external model storage.
Potential architecture:
APK→ code→ lightweight essential assets
External storage:
/sdcard/JARVIS/models/→ LFM→ Qwen→ specialist models
ModelManager should dynamically load external models.
DO NOT create a second ModelManager.

15. CRITICAL PHONE-CRASH INCIDENT
During the latest large Android build attempt:
the entire physical phone crashed after approximately 10 minutes.
This is more serious than a Gradle error.
Potential causes include:

RAM exhaustion
zRAM exhaustion
kernel OOM
swap pressure
Gradle heap
native memory
AAPT2 memory
Box64 overhead
asset compression
filesystem I/O
storage exhaustion
page cache pressure
CPU saturation
thermal throttling
Android process limits
PRoot overhead
multiple native workers
model/build process contention

DO NOT repeat the same heavy build without diagnosing resource pressure.
The goal is maximum safe throughput, not maximum memory utilization.

75. BUILD SYSTEM PROBLEMS AS A WHOLE
The Android build currently has several simultaneous interacting constraints:
ARM64 host+PRoot+large Android project+1.4 GB assets+x86 Build Tools 35+native ARM Build Tools 34+API 36+AGP 8.10+Gradle 8.11.1+NDK+large Kotlin/Compose dependency tree+limited RAM+limited storage+filesystem on shared Android storage+Box64+possibly thermal throttling+long-running asset compression
This means the Android build itself should be considered a resource-managed workload rather than a normal desktop build.

76. POSSIBLE RESOURCE FAILURE MODES
Investigate every one:

physical RAM exhaustion
Android LMK
kernel OOM
zRAM pressure
swap exhaustion
page cache exhaustion
native memory fragmentation
JVM heap
Gradle daemon memory
AAPT2 memory
Box64 memory overhead
PRoot memory overhead
worker concurrency
CPU saturation
thermal throttling
thermal shutdown
storage exhaustion
storage I/O saturation
filesystem lock contention
SD card latency
APK compression workload
Gradle transform cache expansion
build cache expansion
temporary files
/tmp
.gradle
Android SDK cache
Kotlin compiler memory
NDK native compilation
model-related native memory
multiple simultaneous model/build processes
background Android processes


77. SAFE RESOURCE STRATEGY
Potential architecture:
PHONE→ lightweight development/runtime
REMOTE OR SEPARATE BUILDER→ heavy compilation→ model packaging→ huge benchmarks→ large experiments
OR:
PHONE→ Builder admission control→ check RAM→ check storage→ check thermal→ check battery→ determine safe workload→ execute bounded job
Never launch large jobs without resource admission.

confidence

battery

106. SELF-EVOLUTION ALGORITHM

107. MEMORY ALGORITHM
Event→ determine memory-worthiness→ generate candidate→ provenance→ relevance→ conflict analysis→ candidate promotion→ consolidation→ retrieval indexing→ future retrieval

108. CONTEXT ALGORITHM
Task→ classify→ determine context budget→ retrieve relevant memories→ retrieve relevant repository evidence→ retrieve relevant state→ retrieve relevant capabilities→ rank→ compress→ construct prompt→ route model

109. PERFORMANCE ALGORITHM
Before expensive task:
CHECK RAMCHECK CPUCHECK STORAGECHECK THERMALCHECK BATTERYCHECK MODEL RESIDENCYCHECK CONTEXTCHECK CONCURRENCYCHECK DISK
Then:
ADMITorDEFERorREMOTE

120. MOST IMPORTANT STRATEGIC CONCLUSION
The JARVIS project does NOT have an idea shortage.
It has:
INTEGRATION COMPLEXITY+RESOURCE LIMITATIONS+BUILD INFRASTRUCTURE INSTABILITY
The correct strategy is therefore:
STOP EXPANDING
then:
STABILIZE→ VERIFY→ INTEGRATE→ MEASURE→ BENCHMARK→ EVOLVE

121. FIRST PRIORITY
Do NOT immediately run another dangerous Android build.
First determine:

Why the phone crashed.
RAM/zRAM/swap state.
thermal state.
storage free space.
Gradle/PRoot process memory.
asset compression cost.
AAPT2 architecture/version.
correct API-36 toolchain.
whether the 1.28 GB Qwen APK asset is actually required.
whether heavy Android compilation should move to a separate Builder worker.

Then redesign the construction path accordingly.

122. FINAL DESIGN PRINCIPLE
JARVIS should eventually operate as:
STABLE IDENTITY+HUMANCORE+SHARED MEMORY+CANONICAL COGNITION+MODEL ROUTING+CAPABILITY FABRIC+EXECUTION+RESEARCH+BUILDER+EVALUATION+SELF-REPAIR+SAFE EVOLUTION+RESOURCE GOVERNANCE+OBSERVABILITY
The construction system itself should become:
RESOURCE-AWARE+ISOLATED+BOUNDED+REVERSIBLE+PROVENANCE-DRIVEN
The final objective remains:
JARVIS becomes a coherent organism capable of eventually taking responsibility for its own continued construction.

You:
X10THINK
This document doesn't change the diagnosis — it's the same problem with much more surrounding context. Per your own conclusion in it (§95, §120): this isn't an idea shortage, it's integration + build infrastructure + resource limits. Stop expanding, stabilize the build. That's still: Termux native (no PRoot) + patched aapt2 jar, models out of assets/, CI as fallback — from the last message. Nothing in the new inventory contradicts that.
Two real new facts buried in here that I hadn't accounted for and do change your next commands:
1. ./gradlew will fail on this repo regardless of toolchain fix. Because the repo lives on /sdcard (FUSE), the executable bit on gradlew doesn't survive. Always:
bash ./gradlew clean assembleDebug --no-daemon

not ./gradlew ... — this will look like a permissions bug, it isn't, don't waste time chasing it.
2. The AAPT2-34-vs-API-36 failure needs one more check before you retry. You have android-36/android.jar (27,768,026 bytes, confirmed present) but no android-35 or android-34 platform jars installed at all. AAPT2 34 failing to "load include path" against android-36's jar could mean AAPT2 34 genuinely can't parse a 36-level jar — but before concluding that, install the matching platform for whichever build-tools you end up using:
sdkmanager "platforms;android-34"

Then test AAPT2 34 against platforms/android-34/android.jar specifically, not the 36 one. If that resolves cleanly, the real fix is either downgrading compileSdk to 34 (only if nothing in your Kotlin/Compose code needs 36 APIs — check before doing this) or getting a native ARM64 aapt2 that's actually rated for 36 (the Termux pkg install aapt package from last message is more likely to be current/36-compatible than the SDK's bundled 34.0.4).
On §121's "first priority" checklist — you already have the answer, you don't need to run new diagnostics: the crash mechanism doesn't matter precisely because the fix (get build off a fragile ARM64+PRoot+Box64+1.4GB-asset stack) is correct regardless of which resource exhausted first. Yeah — that lines up. What you probably had working before was plain Termux, no PRoot/Ubuntu at all. Termux itself is native ARM64/Bionic, so there's no x86-64 mismatch to fight if you use Termux's own toolchain instead of the SDK's downloaded one. PRoot+Ubuntu+Box64 (what you're doing now) is a heavier, unrelated setup — it's likely why this attempt is unstable when the old one wasn't.
The actual trick: Gradle auto-downloads an aapt2-<version>-linux.jar from Maven that bundles an x86-64 binary. You don't need Box64 or a wrapper script — you replace the binary inside that jar with a native ARM64 one, so Gradle still sees its normal auto-resolved AAPT2 (no "custom AAPT2 location" rejection from AGP, because you're not pointing it anywhere custom).
# 1. Stay in plain Termux — do NOT proot-distro login
pkg update && pkg upgrade -y
pkg install openjdk-17 gradle aapt wget unzip git -y

# 2. Confirm you got a native ARM64 aapt2
file $(which aapt2)
# should say: ELF 64-bit LSB pie executable, ARM aarch64

# 3. Find the x86-64 jar(s) Gradle already cached and patch them in place
find ~/.gradle -name 'aapt2-*-linux.jar' -type f | xargs -I{} \
  jar -u -f {} -C $(dirname $(which aapt2)) aapt2

# 4. Remove any custom override — you don't need it with this method
sed -i '/android.aapt2FromMavenOverride/d' gradle.properties

# 5. Build without the daemon (lower persistent memory footprint)
./gradlew clean assembleDebug --no-daemon

If pkg install aapt doesn't pull a working aapt2 on your Termux version, rendiix/termux-aapt on GitHub is a maintained fallback package with the same binary.
Two things this doesn't fix, and you should still do them:

It removes the PRoot/Box64 overhead layer, but the 1.4GB GGUF still gets run through compressDebugAssets if it's still sitting in mobile/app/src/main/assets/models/ — that's an independent OOM/crash risk regardless of toolchain. Before retrying a full build, temporarily move that file out of assets/, confirm the build succeeds clean, then do the external-storage ModelManager fix from before so it never needs to be back in assets/ at all.
Your JARVIS build history already has a working GitHub Actions pipeline (build #77 green) — this Termux method is a legitimate lighter local option, but if it's flaky again, CI is still the fallback with zero device risk.

Try that in plain Termux first, and pull the model file out of assets/ before the first test build — that isolates whether the toolchain fix alone gets you a clean build, separate from the asset-size risk.
1. Root cause of the crash
You can't fully diagnose a phone that hard-crashed without kernel logs (no root = no dmesg/last_kmsg access), but the architecture itself explains it without needing to guess further:

PRoot Ubuntu + Box64 (emulated x86-64 AAPT2) + Gradle daemon + native NDK toolchain, all stacked, are already 3-4x the CPU/memory overhead of a normal build.
compressDebugAssets was told to gzip/deflate a 1.28GB already-compressed GGUF file (quantized model weights are near-random entropy — zero compression benefit, pure wasted CPU+RAM while holding the whole blob in the JVM heap).
The repo lives on /sdcard (FUSE-mounted on Android), bind-mounted into PRoot — every file op crosses a FUSE bridge plus a PRoot syscall-interception layer. Under sustained I/O + memory pressure this is exactly the kind of path that stalls the kernel's memory reclaim and can trigger a watchdog reset, which reads to you as "the whole phone crashed."
Storage was already at ~9.5GB free with active builds writing multi-GB intermediate artifacts into that same constrained space.

You don't need to isolate exactly which of these tipped it over (OOM-kill cascade vs. I/O stall vs. watchdog reset) — the fix is the same regardless: stop doing this class of build on the phone at all.
2. You already solved this problem once — and reverted
Your own JARVIS history shows the mobile app build pipeline was already fixed: GitHub Actions CI builds only, confirmed green through build #77, with the explicit rule "Termux/local never compiles." What you're describing now — Termux → PRoot Ubuntu → local Gradle/AAPT2/Box64 — is a return to exactly the approach that was abandoned for this reason. This isn't a new problem needing a new architecture; it's the old, already-solved problem happening again because the build moved back onto the device.
First action: stop local Android builds. Push source changes to GitHub, let Actions build it (native x86-64 runners — no PRoot, no Box64, no AAPT2 ARM64 mismatch, no phone RAM/storage limits, real machines with 7GB+ RAM and 14GB+ disk, free tier). This alone eliminates sections A, E, F, and most of B/D — that whole toolchain mismatch simply doesn't exist on a native x86-64 runner.
3. The asset problem is real and separate — fix it regardless of where you build
Packaging a 1.28GB model into every debug APK is wrong on any build host, CI included (slow builds, huge artifacts, no reason for it). Fix:

Confirm the GGUF files are not tracked in git — git ls-files | grep gguf from the repo. If they are, remove them from the working tree and add to .gitignore (and from history via git filter-repo if repo size matters, separately from this build fix).
mobile/app/src/main/assets/models/ should not exist as a packaged path at all. Models live at /sdcard/JARVIS/models/ (matches what you've already started doing) and nowhere in assets/.
In ModelManager, add/confirm a resolution path that checks external storage first:

val modelsDir = File(context.getExternalFilesDir(null), "../../../JARVIS/models")
// or, for shared external storage (needs MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE or scoped-storage-compliant path):
val modelsDir = File(Environment.getExternalStorageDirectory(), "JARVIS/models")

Load .gguf paths from there; if a model file is missing, fail gracefully (log + fallback model or disabled feature) rather than crashing. Don't build a new loader — this is a path change inside the existing ModelManager, not new infrastructure.

Keep only the small STT/TTS/VAD/wake-word models (Sherpa ONNX, Vosk, Silero — these are tens of MB, not GB) in assets/ if they're genuinely required at first launch. Everything Qwen/LFM-sized goes external.

4. compileSdk/targetSdk 36
Not relevant to the crash — this only matters for the AAPT2/ARM64 mismatch, which disappears entirely once builds run on CI. No need to downgrade it.
5. The phone's actual role going forward

Phone: JARVIS runtime, UI, on-device inference via llama-server (already working per your own build log), Python-level Builder tasks that don't require native compilation.
GitHub Actions: all Android/Gradle/AAPT2 compilation, always. This is your "Builder Lab" for the Android body specifically — disposable, remote, resets every run, doesn't touch phone storage or RAM.
Don't add a second remote build service (self-hosted runner, cloud VM, etc.) — you already have a working free one.

6. Concrete next steps, in order

Kill any local Gradle daemons: pkill -f gradle inside the PRoot Ubuntu shell, don't run another local build.
git status in /workspace — check what local changes exist from the AAPT2 debugging (gradle.properties overrides, wrapper edits). Revert gradle.properties AAPT2 override lines — they're CI-irrelevant.
Verify/remove the GGUF from assets/ and git tracking.
Wire the ModelManager external-storage path if not already done.
Commit, push, let build-app.yml run.
Pull the resulting APK from the Actions artifact, install on-device, test.

Nothing here deletes source, .git, or requires new frameworks — it's reverting to your own established rule plus finishing the model-externalization work you'd already started.
That's the whole answer above — the short version: you already fixed this once with GitHub Actions CI, and this local PRoot/Box64 build path is a regression back into the problem you solved before. Stop building on the phone, push to CI, and pull the GGUF out of assets/ so it loads from /sdcard/JARVIS/models/ instead.

Yes. I searched the web specifically for your exact class of problem rather than just generic Android build advice, and the evidence changes the conclusion in a useful way.
What I found
There is an open-source project built specifically for Android APK builds on ARM64 devices using Termux + proot-distro + Ubuntu + Box64. Its documented solution is to patch the AAPT2 binary inside the AGP/Maven aapt2-*-linux.jar cache, not to point android.aapt2FromMavenOverride at arbitrary wrappers. It also explicitly says its ARM64 fallback AAPT2 34.0.4 is only suitable up to compileSdk 34. 
There is also independent Termux build documentation showing that a native AArch64 AAPT2 can be supplied through the Gradle AAPT2 override, including a Build Tools 35.0.2 ARM64 setup. 
Another independent Termux guide uses exactly the technique your situation needs: let Gradle download its normal aapt2-*-linux.jar, then replace the binary inside the cached JAR with a native ARM64 AAPT2. 
And there is an AAPT2 project that builds arm64-v8a AAPT2 from AOSP source, with NDK 27.3.13750724, so a genuinely ARM64 Build-Tools-generation AAPT2 is technically possible rather than relying on the old 34.0.4 binary. 
For CI, the current GitHub-hosted Ubuntu images include Android Build Tools 35/36/37, Android 36, and your exact NDK 27.3.13750724, which makes them a much cleaner build environment than the phone.

cat > /tmp/jarvis_ci_migration_audit.sh <<'AUDIT'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -u

ROOT=/workspace
REPORT=/sdcard/Download/JARVIS_CI_MIGRATION_AUDIT.txt

exec > >(tee "$REPORT") 2>&1

cd "$ROOT" || exit 1

echo "======================================================"
echo " JARVIS CI MIGRATION AUDIT"
echo "======================================================"
date
echo

echo "===== 01 REPOSITORY ====="
pwd
git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || true
git branch --show-current 2>/dev/null || true
git status --short --branch 2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== 02 REMOTES ====="
git remote -v 2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== 03 RECENT COMMITS ====="
git log --oneline -12 2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== 04 WORKFLOWS ====="
find .github/workflows -maxdepth 2 -type f -print 2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== 05 WORKFLOW CONTENT ====="
for f in .github/workflows/*; do
    [ -f "$f" ] || continue
    echo
    echo "----- $f -----"
    sed -n '1,260p' "$f"
done

echo
echo "===== 06 GRADLE FILES ====="
find . -maxdepth 4 \
  \( -name 'settings.gradle' -o \
     -name 'settings.gradle.kts' -o \
     -name 'build.gradle' -o \
     -name 'build.gradle.kts' -o \
     -name 'gradle.properties' -o \
     -name 'gradle-wrapper.properties' \
  \) \
  -not -path './.git/*' \
  -not -path '*/build/*' \
  -print

echo
echo "===== 07 ANDROID CONFIG ====="
grep -RInE \
  'com\.android\.application|compileSdk|targetSdk|minSdk|ndkVersion|aapt2FromMavenOverride|org\.gradle\.jvmargs|org\.gradle\.workers|org\.gradle\.parallel' \
  build.gradle.kts \
  build.gradle \
  gradle.properties \
  mobile \
  2>/dev/null | head -300

echo
echo "===== 08 GRADLE WRAPPER ====="
grep -n 'distributionUrl' gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties 2>/dev/null || true
ls -lh gradlew gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar 2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== 09 GGUF IN REPOSITORY ====="
find . -type f \
  \( -name '*.gguf' -o -name '*.ggml' \) \
  -not -path './.git/*' \
  -not -path '*/build/*' \
  -printf '%s %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
  | sort -nr | head -100

echo
echo "===== 10 TRACKED MODEL FILES ====="
git ls-files | grep -Ei \
  '\.(gguf|ggml|onnx|safetensors|bin)$' \
  2>/dev/null | head -300 || true

echo
echo "===== 11 MAIN ANDROID ASSETS ====="
du -sh mobile/app/src/main/assets 2>/dev/null || true
find mobile/app/src/main/assets -type f \
  -printf '%s %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
  | sort -nr | head -100

echo
echo "===== 12 APK OUTPUTS ====="
find mobile/app/build/outputs -type f \
  -printf '%s %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
  | sort -nr | head -50

echo
echo "===== 13 MODEL MANAGER ====="
find . -type f \
  \( -iname '*ModelManager*' -o \
     -iname '*model*manager*' \) \
  -not -path './.git/*' \
  -not -path '*/build/*' \
  -print 2>/dev/null

echo
echo "===== 14 MODEL LOADING REFERENCES ====="
grep -RInE \
  'ModelManager|llama|gguf|models/|getExternalFilesDir|Environment\.getExternalStorageDirectory|filesDir|assets\.open|AssetManager' \
  mobile/app/src/main \
  runtime \
  core \
  2>/dev/null | head -500 || true

echo
echo "===== 15 CI / BUILD REFERENCES ====="
grep -RInE \
  'build-app|assembleDebug|assembleRelease|upload-artifact|setup-android|setup-gradle|setup-java|android-sdk|sdkmanager' \
  .github \
  scripts \
  docs \
  2>/dev/null | head -500 || true

echo
echo "===== 16 BUILDER REFERENCES TO GRADLE PROPERTIES ====="
grep -RInE \
  'gradle\.properties|aapt2FromMavenOverride|ndk\.dir|build-tools/37|build-tools/35|build-tools/34' \
  building \
  scripts \
  core \
  runtime \
  2>/dev/null | head -500 || true

echo
echo "===== 17 GITIGNORE ====="
cat .gitignore 2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== 18 LFS ====="
git lfs ls-files 2>/dev/null || true
git lfs status 2>/dev/null || true

echo
echo "===== 19 REPOSITORY SIZE ====="
du -sh . 2>/dev/null || true
du -sh .git 2>/dev/null || true
du -sh vault/just_downloaded 2>/dev/null || true
du -sh building 2>/dev/null || true
du -sh mobile/app/build 2>/dev/null || true

echo "===== 20 PHONE STORAGE ====="

echo
echo "===== 21 EXTERNAL MODEL STORAGE ====="
find /sdcard -maxdepth 4 -type f \
  \( -name '*.gguf' -o -name '*.onnx' -o -name '*.safetensors' -o -name '*.bin' \) \
  -printf '%s %p\n' 2>/dev/null \
  | sort -nr | head -100

echo
echo "===== 22 PYTHON BUILDER TEST ====="
python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_building_system.py 2>&1 || true

echo "===== 23 LLAMA HEALTH ====="

The audit exposed a bigger issue than the build: this checkout currently has no Git remote and no .github/workflows at all. So the “CI build #77” belongs to an earlier repository state or a different remote; we should not assume it still exists.
We also found the exact model path problem: ModelManager is already the canonical authority, but LocalInferenceEngine still falls back to filesDir/assets, while runtime/models/model_registry.json points at old repository paths. That is now our next integration target.


ChatGPT:
We have a much clearer picture now. Do not run another Android build yet.

C. Only then create the canonical remote Android Builder

There is no point designing a GitHub Actions workflow until we know which repository/remote this checkout belongs to.
1. First: make the repository trustworthy again
Your audit hit:
fatal: detected dubious ownership in repository at '/workspace'

So Git could not even tell us which files are tracked.
Run this:
cd /workspace

git config --global --add safe.directory /workspace

echo "===== GIT IDENTITY ====="
git rev-parse --show-toplevel
git branch --show-current
git status --short --branch
echo

echo "===== REMOTES ====="
git remote -v
echo

echo "===== HEAD ====="
git rev-parse HEAD 2>/dev/null || true
git log -1 --oneline --decorate 2>/dev/null || true
echo

echo "===== TRACKED LARGE FILES ====="
git ls-files -z | while IFS= read -r -d '' f; do
    case "$f" in
        *.gguf|*.ggml|*.onnx|*.safetensors|*.bin)
            [ -f "$f" ] && stat -c '%s %n' "$f"
            ;;
    esac
done | sort -nr | head -100

echo
echo "===== GIT STATUS ====="
git status --short --branch

Do not commit or reset anything yet.
We need that output because right now we don't know whether the 1.28 GB GGUF is actually tracked.

2. Second: snapshot every local change before touching the model system

Create a non-destructive inventory:
cd /workspace

This gives us a rollback/reference point without touching source.

↓
Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_K_M.gguf

That means the model wasn't sitting in assets/ accidentally. The current implementation actually expects that fallback.

 ↓

LocalInferenceEngine

 ↓
Model source resolver
 ├── external model storage

 ├── llama-server
 └── deterministic fallback

One canonical ModelManager.
No second model system.

Why this is the next technical step
Because we now know something important:
ModelManager is already intended to be the sole authority.
The code itself says:

"ModelManager — orchestrates model providers"

and:

"ModelManager is the SOLE model..."

That means the previous architectural plan was correct. We don't need to invent a model-storage subsystem.
We need to extend the existing authority with a proper model-source resolver.

5. The storage architecture we are aiming for
I would make the hierarchy:
ModelManager
    │
    ├── llama.cpp server
    │
    ├── local native inference
    │
    ├── external model file
    │
    ├── app-private copied model
    │
    └── controlled fallback

For a local GGUF:
1. Explicit configured path
        ↓
2. /sdcard/JARVIS/models/
        ↓
3. app external files/models/
        ↓
4. app internal files/models/
        ↓
5. APK assets ONLY for small emergency/bootstrap assets

The 1.28 GB Qwen file should not be in step 5.

6. There is another important discovery
Your current repository has:
runtime/models/model_registry.json

with old paths like:
/sdcard/jarvis-repo/vault/just_downloaded/LFM...
/sdcard/jarvis-repo/vault/just_downloaded/Qwen...

Those paths are no longer authoritative.
That's dangerous because we could end up with:
ModelManager
→ one model path

runtime registry
→ another model path

scripts/jarvis-model-manager.sh
→ another model path

Android LocalInferenceEngine
→ another model path

That violates your one model authority principle.
So the model migration needs to synchronize:
Android ModelManager
+
runtime model registry
+
llama.cpp controller
+
external storage layout

instead of fixing only the APK.
