      "QWEN Q8" \
      "$MODEL_ROOT/Qwen3-1.7B-Q8_0.gguf" \
      "/sdcard/jarvis-repo/logs_qwen_q8_three_brain.txt" \
      "QWEN Q8 ONLINE"

      -printf '%s %p\n' \
      | sort -nr

    echo "===== 13 APK ASSET CHECK ====="

      -type f \
      \( -name '*.gguf' -o -name '*.ggml' \) \
      -print -quit 2>/dev/null | grep -q .; then
        echo "FAIL: model still inside APK assets"
        exit 1
    else
        echo "PASS: no GGUF/GGML in APK assets"
    fi

    echo
    echo "===== 14 STORAGE ====="
    df -h /sdcard

    echo
    echo "===== 15 GIT RELEVANT STATUS ====="
    git status --short \
      -- .gitignore \
      runtime/models/model_registry.json \

      scripts/jarvis-model-manager.sh \
      mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model

    echo "======================================================"
    echo " THREE-BRAIN SETUP VERIFIED"
    echo "======================================================"

  } > "$OUT" 2>&1

  RC=$?

  echo
  echo "EXPORT: $OUT"
  ls -lh "$OUT"

  exit $RC
}

NDK 27.3.13750724
CMake 3.22.1
platform-tools/ADB

NDK:
 /root/android-sdk/ndk/27.3.13750724

Android platform:
 /root/android-sdk/platforms/android-36

AAPT2:
 /root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2
is native AArch64 and executes.

AAPT2:
 /root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2
is x86-64 and does NOT directly execute on ARM64.

Box64 exists:

/usr/bin/box64

Box64 can run the x86-64 Android build tool when invoked explicitly.

============================================================
7. STORAGE PROBLEM
============================================================

Phone is storage constrained.

Latest approximate storage:
106 GB total
~91-94 GB used
~13-16 GB free depending on build cleanup

Historical large usage:
- .git ~6.1 GB
- vault/just_downloaded ~1.3-5.3 GB depending state
- mobile/app/build ~1.9-2.1 GB
- PRoot storage ~8.5 GB
- ~/.npm ~2.1 GB
- ~/.gradle ~763 MB+

A previous build caused the entire phone to crash after ~10 minutes.

Likely contributors:
- PRoot overhead
- Box64
- Android/NDK build
- FUSE / /sdcard I/O
- Gradle
- native compilation
- huge GGUF asset packaging
- ~1.28 GB GGUF inside APK assets
- storage pressure
- high sustained CPU/RAM/I/O

Exact hard-crash mechanism was not proven because root/kernel crash logs weren't available.

Important conclusion:
Do not return to "giant local Android build" as the normal architecture.

Preferred long-term:
- phone = JARVIS runtime + lightweight local inference
- CI = Android build laboratory
- disposable Builder Lab = heavy construction resources

============================================================
8. ANDROID BUILD HISTORY
============================================================

Initial environment:
- Android SDK missing
- NDK missing
- sdkmanager missing
- aapt2/d8/apksigner/aidl missing

Termux mirror was repaired with:
termux-change-repo

Chosen mirror:
mirrors.tuna.tsinghua.edu.cn

Installed initially:
android-tools
aapt2
d8
apksigner
aidl
Java 21 dependency

Later a specialized ARM64/PRoot Android builder provided:
- Android platform 36
- ARM64 build-tools 34.0.4
- x86-64 build-tools 35.0.0 via Box64
- NDK
- platform-tools

Android project stack was updated from:
AGP 8.7.0
Gradle 8.9
implicit NDK 27.0.12077973

to:
AGP 8.10.0
Gradle 8.11.1
explicit NDK 27.3.13750724
compileSdk 36
targetSdk 36
minSdk 26
CMake 3.22.1

This was successful enough to progress substantially through the build.

However:

Gradle 8.11.1 produces a Kotlin plugin warning that future Kotlin 2.5 requires Gradle 8.14.4.
This is currently a warning, not the principal blocker.

============================================================
9. AAPT2 PROBLEM
============================================================

Original gradle.properties contained:

android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/37.0.0/aapt2

That path did not exist.

Then:
35.0.0 AAPT2 was tested but is x86-64.

Direct:
 /root/android-sdk/build-tools/35.0.0/aapt2
fails on ARM64 with:
cannot execute: required file not found

34.0.4 is native AArch64:

Android Asset Packaging Tool
(aapt) 2.19-S.1ca575d_5b9-11cfbb

Using:
android.aapt2FromMavenOverride=/root/android-sdk/build-tools/34.0.4/aapt2

allowed Gradle to get farther but later:
AAPT:
failed to load include path
/root/android-sdk/platforms/android-36/android.jar

AAPT2 35 can be executed explicitly through Box64.

Several wrapper/launcher attempts were rejected by AGP because the custom AAPT2 location was not recognized as an executable.

Important conclusion:
DO NOT keep inventing AAPT2 wrappers.
Do not spend the project's future on this phone-native Android build issue.

CI is the preferred Android build mechanism.

~1.9 GB reclaimed.

============================================================
16. CURRENT MODEL SYSTEM
============================================================

THERE ARE THREE LOCAL AI ROLES.

Do NOT collapse this architecture to "LFM + Qwen".

BRAIN 1:
LFM2.5-1.2B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf

Approx:
698-730 MB

Role:
- fast local cognition
- routine cognition
- classification
- routing
- context compression
- general fast fallback
- background cognition

Verified working.

Smoke result:
"LFM ONLINE"

Observed:
~2.45 generated tok/s in one test
~9.75 prompt tok/s
load successfully

BRAIN 2:
Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_K_M.gguf

Approx:
1.28 GB

Role:
- normal/deeper reasoning
- code diagnosis
- planning
- research synthesis
- repair hypotheses
- Builder reasoning

Verified working.

Observed:
~1.41 generated tok/s
~7.73 prompt tok/s

BRAIN 3:
Qwen3-1.7B-Q8_0.gguf

Approx:
1.83 GB

Role:
- deepest local reasoning
- higher quality reasoning candidate
- difficult Builder/research/system tasks
- deliberate reasoning

Verified working.

Observed:
~1.24 generated tok/s
~9.02 prompt tok/s

Q8 is slower than Q4 on this CPU in the observed test.

Therefore:
DO NOT blindly make Q8 default.

The model router should eventually choose based on:
- task complexity
- latency requirement
- confidence
- RAM
- CPU
- thermal pressure
- battery
- context length
- task class
- model availability

============================================================
19. MODEL PATH RESOLVER
============================================================

Created:

mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/storage/ModelPathResolver.kt

Purpose:
single model file resolution authority.

Resolution order:

1. explicit absolute filesystem path
2. canonical shared model vault
3. app-specific external model directory
4. app-private model directory

Canonical root:

/sdcard/jarvis-repo/models

Aliases:

lfm2.5-1.2b-instruct
→ LFM2.5-1.2B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf

lfm
→ LFM2.5-1.2B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf

qwen3-1.7b
→ Qwen3-1.7B-Q8_0.gguf

qwen3-1.7b-q8
→ Qwen3-1.7B-Q8_0.gguf

qwen3-1.7b-q4
→ Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_K_M.gguf

qwen3-1.7b-q4_k_m
→ Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_K_M.gguf

Unknown plain .gguf filename is accepted if safe filename format.

A configured endpoint such as:
127.0.0.1:8080

is NOT treated as a filesystem model path.

APK assets intentionally are NOT used as GGUF fallback.

IMPORTANT CURRENT INCONSISTENCY:
The resolver currently defaults qwen3-1.7b to Qwen3-1.7B-Q8_0.gguf, while some runtime controller logic still uses Qwen Q4 for "qwen".
This must be rationalized.

22. CURRENT HTTP LLAMA-CPP ADAPTER
============================================================

mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/adapters/LlamaCppAdapter.kt

HTTP adapter for:
llama-server

Base URL:
http://127.0.0.1:8080

Uses:
- /health
- /v1/chat/completions

Supports:
- non-streaming
- streaming
- JSON messages
- tools schema payloads
- health
- model info

Current limitations:
- dynamic model loading not supported
- dynamic unloading not supported
- token usage not directly tracked
- tools are not actually fully parsed into structured ToolCall results
- model metadata is partly hardcoded

This adapter still exists alongside LocalInferenceEngine.

DO NOT automatically delete it.

Need to decide architecture:
either HTTP llama-server is temporary operational substrate,
or LocalInferenceEngine is the canonical Android-native runtime,
or ModelManager supports both as different runtime organs.

The project philosophy prefers provider-neutral ModelManager.

IMPORTANT MAJOR GAP:

ModelManager currently initializes:

_activeProvider = heuristicAdapter

So a fresh ModelManager starts in HEURISTIC.

Historically this passed tests because tests deliberately expect heuristic as deterministic offline startup.

This means ModelManager is NOT YET VERIFIED as the real three-brain authority.

This is one of the highest-priority integration gaps.

- provider switching
- provider configuration
- model load/unload
- health sweeps

- streaming
- status

- retry logic
- requestChat
- sendWithContext
- periodic health checks

It is intended to be the SOLE model authority.

But:
it currently does not cleanly implement the full three-model router yet.

============================================================
30. SMALL MODEL OPTIMIZATION
============================================================

Core principle:

MORE RELEVANT TOKENS > MORE TOKENS.

Do not solve small-model limitations by dumping the repository into context.

Instead:

TASK
→ retrieve relevant evidence
→ rank
→ compress
→ construct task-specific context
→ model

Mechanisms:
- retrieval
- context budgeting
- task-specific context
- memory retrieval
- repository indexing
- code slicing
- capability retrieval
- state retrieval
- context compression
- prompt compilation
- structured outputs
- deterministic rules
- specialist routing
- bounded loops

31. ADAPTIVE CONTEXT BUILDER
============================================================

Existing:

mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/body/AdaptiveContextBuilder.kt

Uses:
- MemoryStore
- VocabularyStore
- LocalInferenceEngine

Default config:
max context = 4096
max prompt = 3000
max memory = 1500
max conversation = 1000
max vocabulary = 200

Weights:
recency .4
relevance .4
importance .2

Threshold:
.3

Compression:
enabled
ratio .5

Pipeline:
1. conversation context
2. relevant memories
3. vocabulary hints
4. user profile
5. system context
6. rank sections
7. apply token budget
8. add current turn
9. verify token count

Resource pressure:
up to 70% reduction of context budgets.

Memory ranking:
composite:
recency
+ relevance
+ importance

35. HUMANCORE
============================================================

HumanCore is a major existing subsystem.

- identity kernel

- personality
- emotional intelligence
- emotional regulation
- bond formation

- trust
- values

filesDir/humancore/

HumanCore is initialized by JarvisEngine.

HumanCore ModelPort is supposed to call the canonical model authority.

- HumanCore

- VoiceOrganismHost
- VoiceOutputPath
- VoiceMicroSystem
- LanguageRouter
- MemoryStore
- StreamingTts
- VisualStateBridge
- RuntimeBinder
- LiquidEnvironmentManager
- BootstrapManager

Important architecture:
ModelManager + shared stores are process-scoped singletons.

JarvisEngine initializes ModelBackend.requestChat to route to:
modelManager.requestChat(...)

Thus HumanCore's model seam can reach ModelManager.

JarvisEngine also:
- loads capability manifest
- initializes CompanionCore
- initializes BodyCoordinator
- initializes voice habitat
- registers voice capability
- initializes LatencyLayer
- initializes RuntimeBinder
- initializes EnvironmentManager
- initializes BootstrapManager

============================================================
43. BUILDER MUTANT ENVIRONMENT
============================================================

Existing MutantEnvironment does:
- create isolated workspace
- copy Gradle wrapper/tooling
- copy gradle/
- write implementation
- write genome
- create manifest
- build candidate
- store build artifacts
- test candidate

It uses:
repo_root = /sdcard/jarvis-repo

Because /sdcard does not preserve executable bit:
Builder runs Gradle wrapper through bash.

Use:
bash ./gradlew

NOT:
./gradlew

This is a FUSE/executable-bit issue, not a Gradle problem.

============================================================
44. BUILDER FAILURES / PHASE 12 HISTORY
============================================================

Failure 1:
Gradle wrapper permission denied.

Cause:
Android /sdcard mount doesn't preserve executable bit.

Fix:
bash ./gradlew ...

Failure 2:
Gradle test task wasn't found inside a mutant.

Manual tasks worked.

Likely environment/workspace/project discovery issue.

Another self-development result:
- build succeeded
- test failed
- observation succeeded
- CPU regression
- fitness 0.53
- threshold 0.6
- candidate rejected

DO NOT lower threshold merely to make it pass.

Baseline metrics were placeholders:
startup_time_ms = 500
memory_mb = 200
cpu_percent = 5
health_check_latency_ms = 50
quality = .9

These need live measurements later.

============================================================
57. CODING AGENT DECISIONS
============================================================

Researched:
- Claude Code
- OpenCode
- Cline
- Codex CLI
- Gemini CLI
- Antigravity CLI
- Aider
- Pi
- Crush
- etc.

Conclusions:
- Cline considered too UI-heavy/unstable
- OpenCode had latency/runtime issues
- Claude Code not desirable as permanent zero-budget dependency
- Crush was a strong fit
- Aider stable
- Pi token-efficient and Termux-friendly

Pi was selected as a TEMPORARY development operator.

Pi is NOT final JARVIS brain.

Eventually:
JARVIS Builder replaces external coding agent dependency.

============================================================
71. IMPORTANT MODEL INTEGRATION GAP
============================================================

CRITICAL:

The physical models are now present and llama-server can run each.

BUT:
that does NOT yet prove the Android application uses them correctly.

Known current gap:

ModelManager starts with HEURISTIC.

LocalInferenceEngine exists but ModelManager has not yet been proven to use it as canonical live provider.

LlamaCppAdapter exists and uses HTTP.

The project currently has TWO local model approaches:
1. llama-server HTTP
2. Android-native JNI

Need one coherent policy.

Recommended near-term:
use llama-server as the stable operational runtime for the phone,
while keeping LocalInferenceEngine as the native local inference organ/experimental path until benchmarked.

Do not create duplicate final model authorities.

ModelManager should become the authority.

cd /workspace

echo "===== MODEL REGISTRY ====="
cat runtime/models/model_registry.json

sed -n '1,260p' runtime/model_gateway/model_controller.py

echo
echo "===== MODEL MANAGER ====="
sed -n '1,320p' scripts/jarvis-model-manager.sh

echo
echo "===== SIMPLE MODEL SCRIPT ====="
sed -n '1,320p' scripts/jarvis-model.sh

echo "===== ANDROID MODEL PACKAGE ====="
find mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model -type f -maxdepth 10 -print

echo
echo "===== COGNITIVE PACKAGE ====="
find mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/cognitive -type f -maxdepth 10 -print | sort

echo
echo "===== RUNTIME MODEL GATEWAY ====="
find runtime -maxdepth 3 -type f | sort

Do NOT modify before this inspection.

============================================================
74. IMPORTANT CURRENT CONTRADICTIONS
============================================================

Contradiction 1:
Model registry has LFM + Qwen Q4 + Qwen Q8.

But current model_controller may only expose:
lfm
qwen(Q4)

Contradiction 2:
ModelPathResolver:
qwen3-1.7b
→ Qwen Q8

But runtime controller's "qwen"
→ Qwen Q4

Need explicit model IDs.

Contradiction 3:
ModelManager's LLAMA_CPP adapter is LlamaCppAdapter HTTP.

LocalInferenceEngine also claims ModelProviderType.LLAMA_CPP.

This creates two possible concrete providers for the same enum.

Need rationalize this.

Contradiction 4:
ModelManager starts in heuristic.

This is acceptable for deterministic unit tests but not as the final production cognitive authority.

Contradiction 5:
The current InferencePolicyLayer classifies tier but does not yet directly choose the three models.

Contradiction 6:
The cognitive gateway exists conceptually/partially but live Android conversation path still requires verification.

============================================================
75. LLAMA SERVER CURRENT FACTS
============================================================

llama-server:
- working
- aarch64
- CPU only
- port 8080
- local loopback
- model hot-swap done by stopping/restarting process
- n_ctx used in smoke tests as 1024
- normal scripts target 4096
- n_slots = 1
- n_gpu_layers = 0

LFM:
~2.45 tok/s generation

Qwen Q4:
~1.41 tok/s generation

Qwen Q8:
~1.24 tok/s generation

These numbers are phone/runtime observations, not universal benchmark claims.

Need future benchmark matrix.

============================================================
87. CI ROLE
============================================================

Preferred Android build architecture:
phone source
→ Git
→ GitHub Actions
→ Android build
→ APK artifact
→ phone install/test

Why:
- avoids phone RAM/CPU/storage constraints
- avoids PRoot/Box64 AAPT2 complexity
- avoids FUSE build instability
- avoids massive local Gradle builds

There was historical evidence that GitHub Actions had a working green pipeline up to build #77, but the current repository audit showed no workflow files in origin at that moment.

Therefore:
DO NOT assume current CI exists.
VERIFY the current GitHub Actions/workflow state before depending on it.

============================================================

============================================================

Git remote existed.

However current audit showed:
WORKFLOW FILES IN ORIGIN MAIN = none returned.

Therefore:
Do not claim CI is active without checking current repository.

If necessary:
inspect .github/workflows/
inspect GitHub repo
verify actual workflow.

100. FINAL OPERATING RULES FOR THE NEXT AI
============================================================

RULE 1:
Start by reading current repository/code, not old assumptions.

RULE 2:
Use actual execution evidence.

RULE 3:
Do not reset Git.

RULE 4:
Do not delete source blindly.

RULE 5:
Do not re-download models that already exist.

RULE 6:
Do not move the models again.

RULE 7:
Canonical model directory:
 /sdcard/jarvis-repo/models/

RULE 8:
No GGUF inside APK assets.

RULE 9:
Do not treat llama-server smoke tests as proof Android integration is complete.

RULE 10:
Do not treat LocalInferenceEngine existence as proof Android-native inference is complete.

RULE 11:
Do not treat ModelManager existence as proof of real model authority.

RULE 12:
Do not treat CognitiveEngine existence as proof it is the live canonical brain.

RULE 13:
Do not create another model manager.

RULE 14:
Do not create another memory authority.

RULE 15:
Do not create another capability registry.

RULE 16:
Do not create another execution layer.

RULE 17:
Every new capability must enter canonical Capability Fabric.

RULE 18:
Every model remains an organ.

RULE 19:
Every self-modification must be isolated, bounded, evaluated, reversible.

RULE 20:
Use external projects as mechanism donors.

RULE 21:
Do not install external architectures wholesale.

RULE 22:
Prefer retrieval and structured context over huge prompts.

RULE 23:
Prefer exact commands.

RULE 24:
When output is large, provide export commands.

RULE 25:
When environment is unstable, use one step at a time.

RULE 26:
When error occurs, diagnose before changing architecture.

RULE 27:
Do not make the phone a permanent build farm.

RULE 28:
Prefer disposable Builder Lab.

RULE 29:
Do not weaken benchmark/promotion gates.

RULE 30:
Do not call old APK proof.

RULE 31:
Do not call files "implemented" without evidence.

============================================================

JARVIS is an AI-organism project built on Android ARM64.

Current body:
Android
→ Termux
→ temporary PRoot Ubuntu
→ /workspace
→ JARVIS

Current model organs:
- LFM2.5-1.2B-Instruct-Q4_K_M
- Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_K_M
- Qwen3-1.7B-Q8_0

All three are now physically available and verified through llama-server.

Canonical model storage:
 /sdcard/jarvis-repo/models/

The huge GGUF is no longer packaged in the APK.

The project already contains:
- HumanCore
- memory
- cognition
- continuity
- state engine
- capability fabric
- execution
- Builder
- mutant environments
- evolution
- failure/recovery
- voice
- browser/research concepts

- federation
- runtime environment management
- native JNI inference
- HTTP llama.cpp adapter
- context builder
- inference policy layer
- model manager
- model registry
- model controller
- many tests and research mechanisms

The current bottleneck is:
INTEGRATING THEM.

The immediate mission is:

MODEL AUTHORITY
→ COGNITIVE GATEWAY
→ CONTEXT
→ ROUTING
→ THREE LOCAL BRAIN ORGANS
→ HUMANCORE
→ MEMORY
→ CAPABILITY FABRIC

After that:
BUILDER
→ RESEARCH

→ SELF-EVOLUTION
→ RUNTIME SELF-OWNERSHIP

The final principle remains:

+
